# TIMELINE · Dvāpara Yuga

> **Period 5 of 9 on the cosmic clock.** One slice of the corpus-wide [TIMELINE hub](../TIMELINE.md); each entry links to the file where the event is detailed. Back to the [master index](../README.md).
> ← Prev: [Tretā Yuga](03-treta-yuga.md) · [↑ TIMELINE hub](../TIMELINE.md) · Next: [Kali (Scriptural)](05-kali-yuga-scriptural.md) →

**1720 events.**

*Dharma on two legs. Kṛṣṇa and the Mahābhārata.*

---

- **Abdication to the forest** — remorseful and barred from fatherhood, Pāṇḍu renounces the throne to Dhṛtarāṣṭra and retires with Kuntī and Mādrī to ascetic life at Śataśṛṅga _(actors: Pāṇḍu, Kuntī, Mādrī, Dhṛtarāṣṭra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/pandu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Abduction of Draupadī by Jayadratha** — Jayadratha, smitten, carries Draupadī off by force from the Pāṇḍava forest hermitage while the brothers hunt _(actors: Jayadratha, Draupadī, Dhaumya, Kotikāsya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/jayadratha.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva 263–267)
- **Abduction of the Kāśi princesses** — Bhīṣma storms the svayaṃvara of Kāśī and carries off Ambā, Ambikā, and Ambālikā by force as war-brides for Vicitravīrya _(actors: Bhīṣma, Ambikā, Ambālikā, Ambā, Vicitravīrya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ambika-ambalika.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva ~102)
- **Abduction of the three Kāśī princesses** — Bhīṣma seizes Ambā, Ambikā and Ambālikā by force from the Kāśī svayaṃvara as brides for Vicitravīrya, defeating the assembled kings including Śālva _(actors: Bhīṣma, Ambā, Ambikā, Ambālikā, Śālva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/amba-shikhandi.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva ~102; Udyoga Parva ~173)
- **Abhimanyu Born to Arjuna and Subhadra** — Abhimanyu is born at Indraprastha to Arjuna and Subhadra as the incarnation of Varcas son of Soma, fated to return after only sixteen years _(actors: Abhimanyu, Arjuna, Subhadra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/abhimanyu.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi Parva (Sambhava))
- **Acchodā reborn as Kālī/Satyavatī from the fish Adrikā** — The decree detonates: Uparicara Vasu's seed falls into the Yamunā, the cursed apsarā-fish Adrikā bears the dark fisher-girl Kālī (Satyavatī), a king's daughter fostered at the ferry _(actors: Acchodā/Satyavatī, Uparicara Vasu, Adrikā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/acchoda-satyavati-rebirth.md) — (Matsya Purāṇa, 14; Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva ~57)
- **Aditi's earrings and boon** — after the Naraka-vadha Kṛṣṇa flies with Satyabhāmā on Garuḍa to Amarāvatī to restore Aditi's stolen earrings, and Aditi grants Satyabhāmā unfading youth and beauty beside Kṛṣṇa _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Satyabhāmā, Aditi, Garuḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/parijata-harana.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.30; Bhāgavata X.59)
- **Aditi's mediation and Indra's condition** — Aditi reconciles her son with Kṛṣṇa and Indra yields the tree on the condition that it bloom on earth only while the avatāra walks it, returning to Svarga at his departure _(actors: Aditi, Indra, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/parijata-harana.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.30)
- **Akrūra summoned to Vraja** — Kaṃsa sends the devotee-noble Akrūra to fetch Krishna and Balarāma to Mathurā for the bow-sacrifice festival _(actors: Kaṃsa, Akrūra, Krishna, Balarāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kamsa-vadha.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa X.36–38; Harivaṃśa Viṣṇu Parva; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5)
- **Arjuna and Kṛṣṇa proclaimed as Nara-Nārāyaṇa** — Paraśurāma closes the tale by identifying Arjuna as Nara and Kṛṣṇa as Nārāyaṇa, the eternal sages born among men, and urges Duryodhana to make peace before Gāṇḍīva's arrows fall _(actors: Paraśurāma, Arjuna, Kṛṣṇa, Duryodhana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dambhodbhava-nara-narayana.md) — (Mahābhārata CE 5.94, Udyoga Parva, Bhagavad-yāna)
- **Arjuna redeems the five crocodile-apsarās at Mahīsāgara-saṃgama** — on his pilgrimage-exile Arjuna drags cursed crocodile-apsarās from the Mahī–sea confluence and restores their forms, proving the tīrtha's redeeming power _(actors: Arjuna, five apsarās, a cursing Brāhmaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mahisagara-sangama-kumarika-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa; cf. Mbh Ādi Parva)
- **Aśvatthāman leads the Sauptika night massacre** — Consecrated as the last Kaurava senāpati and empowered by Śiva at the camp gate, he slaughters Dhṛṣṭadyumna, the Upapāṇḍavas, Śikhaṇḍin and the sleeping Pāñcāla host _(actors: Aśvatthāman, Śiva, Kṛpa, Kṛtavarma, Dhṛṣṭadyumna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ashvatthama.md) — (Mahābhārata, Sauptika Parva 1-9)
- **Aśvins father Nakula and Sahadeva** — by Kuntī's boon-mantra the twin physician-gods father the twin Pāṇḍavas Nakula and Sahadeva on Mādrī, wife of Pāṇḍu _(actors: Aśvins, Mādrī, Nakula, Sahadeva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ashvins.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Balarāma hears the Sarasvatī fords' legends** — on his war-neutral forty-two-day Sarasvatī pilgrimage, Balarāma visits the Sārasvata and Vṛddhakanyā tīrthas whose upākhyānas are recited to him _(actors: Balarāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sarasvata-upakhyana-vriddhakumari.md) — (Mbh Śalya Parva 9.50–52; Ganguli LI–LII)
- **Balarāma slays the ape Dvivida** — The mountain-hurling gorilla Dvivida, ravaging Ānarta to avenge Naraka, is battered to death by Balarāma on Raivataka hill _(actors: Balarāma, Dvivida)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/krishna-lila.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.67)
- **Balarāma slays the other King Haṃsa** — after an eighteen-day battle Balarāma kills a king who merely shares Haṃsa's name, seeding the fatal false report _(actors: Balarāma, King Haṃsa (homonym))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-dimbhaka.md) — (Mahābhārata, Sabhā Parva 2.14)
- **Balarāma slays the rogue ape Dvivida** — the Aśvin-born vānara, avenging Narakāsura by ravaging Ānarta, is battered to death on Raivataka hill _(actors: Dvivida, Balarāma)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/vanaras-and-rikshas.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.67)
- **Balarāma's serpent-exit departure** — at Prabhāsa after the Yādava fratricide, Balarāma gives up his body in yoga and a great white serpent issues from his mouth into the sea, Saṅkarṣaṇa returning to Śeṣa _(actors: Balarāma, Kṛṣṇa, Ocean, the nāgas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/balarama.md) — (Mahābhārata, Mausala Parva 4; Bhāgavata 11.30)
- **Balarāma's tīrtha-yātrā — Romaharṣaṇa and Balvala** — Sitting out the war, Balarāma tours the sacred fords, kills the unrising sūta Romaharṣaṇa with a kuśa blade at Naimiṣāraṇya, installs Ugraśravas as reciter, and slays the demon Balvala _(actors: Balarāma, Romaharṣaṇa, Ugraśravas, Balvala, the Naimiṣa sages)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/krishna-lila.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.78–79)
- **Belālasena's severed head witnesses Kurukṣetra** — Bhīma's son gives Kṛṣṇa his head before the war, watches all eighteen days, and testifies that only Kṛṣṇa's cakra did the killing [regional] _(actors: Belālasena, Kṛṣṇa, Bhīma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/odia-literature-sarala-and-panchasakha.md) — (Sāraḷā Dāsa, Sāraḷā Mahābhārata)
- **Bhānumatī wedded to Sahadeva** — Nārada reveals Durvāsas' curse fulfilled and discharged, and the rescued maiden is bestowed on Mādrī's son Sahadeva _(actors: Nārada, Bhānumatī, Sahadeva, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nikumbha-bhanumati-shatpura.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva ch. 91, Dutt)
- **Bhīma reaches the Lauhitya** — in the eastern digvijaya Bhīma subdues Prāgjyotiṣa and levies tribute from the mleccha kings beyond the Lauhitya _(actors: Bhīma, Bhagadatta)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lauhitya-brahmaputra-origin.md) — (Mahābhārata, Sabhā Parva)
- **Bhīṣma narrates Mucukunda on the bed of arrows** — dying after Kurukṣetra, the grandsire teaches Yudhiṣṭhira rājadharma through the tale of Mucukunda and Vaiśravaṇa, that a king's sword is empty without a priest's fire _(actors: Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mucukunda-vaishravana-shanti.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva 12.75, Ganguli LXXIV)
- **Bhīṣma teaches the mānasa-tīrthas** — within the arrow-bed instruction cycle, the 'tīrthas of the heart' are taught: truth their water, restraint and compassion their ghāṭs, above all outer fords _(actors: Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tirtha-and-tirtha-yatra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva)
- **Bhīṣma tells the Sṛñjaya story** — dying on his bed of arrows, Bhīṣma answers Yudhiṣṭhira's grief with the old Nārada–Sṛñjaya dialogue, a consolation-inset framing the roll of the Sixteen Kings _(actors: Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira, Nārada, Sṛñjaya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/suvarnashthivin-srinjaya-narada-parvata.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva ~29–31)
- **Bhīṣma tells the Uñchavṛtti-upākhyāna** — on the arrow-bed after Kurukṣetra the dying grandsire closes the Mokṣadharma by narrating to Yudhiṣṭhira the tale of Dharmāraṇya and the Nāga Padmanābha, exalting the gleaner's life as the surest dharma _(actors: Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/unchavritti-brahmin-naga-padmanabha.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva CCCLV–CCCLXV / CE 12.340–353)
- **Bhīṣma's minor Śānti-Parva insets** — from his bed of arrows the dying Bhīṣma answers Yudhiṣṭhira with five small didactic tales on japa, deliberation, dharma-over-wealth, hope, and enmity _(actors: Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shanti-parva-minor-upakhyanas.md) — (MBh, Śānti Parva 12)
- **Bhīṣma's twin varṇa-upākhyānas** — on the bed of arrows Bhīṣma answers Yudhiṣṭhira's questions on caste with two adjacent, opposed tales — Mātaṅga (Brāhmaṇahood refused) and Vītahavya (Brāhmaṇahood conferred) _(actors: Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matanga-vitahavya-varna-upakhyanas.md) — (Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva)
- **Bhīṣma-pañcaka instituted** — Bhīṣma expounds dharma from the arrow-bed through Kārttika's last five days, and Kṛṣṇa charters the five-day fast in his honor _(actors: Bhīṣma, Kṛṣṇa, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/masa-mahatmyas-kartika-magha-vaishakha.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa; Hari-bhakti-vilāsa)
- **Bhūriśravas slays the ten sons of Sātyaki** — In the fighting of the Jayadratha-vadha day Bhūriśravas kills Sātyaki's ten sons, sharpening the feud inherited from Śini and Somadatta _(actors: Bhūriśravas, Sātyaki)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/satyaki.md) — (Mahābhārata, Droṇa Parva)
- **Birth of Aśvatthāman with the forehead gem** — Droṇa and Kṛpī's son is born crying like the horse Uccaiḥśravas, named by a sky-voice, and bearing a protective gem in his forehead _(actors: Aśvatthāman, Droṇa, Kṛpī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ashvatthama.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Birth of Haṃsa and Ḍimbhaka by Śiva's boon** — childless Brahmadatta of Śālva wins two weapon-proof sons after ten years' worship, while his brāhmaṇa friend Mitrasaha gains the Viṣṇu-named Janārdana _(actors: Brahmadatta, Śiva, Haṃsa, Ḍimbhaka, Janārdana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-dimbhaka.md) — (Harivaṃśa vulgate, Bhaviṣya Parva 104)
- **Birth of Nakula and Sahadeva by the Aśvins** — Mādrī, lent Kuntī's mantra once, summons the twin Aśvins and bears twin sons in a single birth on Mount Śataśṛṅga, a bodiless voice declaring they will surpass their fathers in beauty and energy _(actors: Mādrī, Pāṇḍu, Kuntī, the Aśvins, Nakula, Sahadeva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/nakula-sahadeva.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi Parva)
- **Birth of Śuka from the araṇi** — Vyāsa's tapas for a son pure as the five elements bears fruit when, at the sight of the apsarā Ghṛtācī in she-parrot form, his seed falls on the fire-drill sticks and Śuka is born from them, bathed by Gaṅgā in person _(actors: Vyāsa, Śiva, Ghṛtācī, Śuka, Gaṅgā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shuka-shukadeva.md) — (Mahābhārata XII, Mokṣadharma, Śuka-carita)
- **Chandra Tāl claimed as Yudhiṣṭhira's ascent-place** — Himalayan tradition localizes the Mahāprasthānika climax at the Spiti moon-lake, holding that Indra's chariot descended to Chandra Tāl to carry the eldest Pāṇḍava bodily to Svarga (the epic itself names only Meru's slope) _(actors: Yudhiṣṭhira, Indra)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/chandra-taal-yudhishthira-svargarohana.md) — (Mahābhārata, Mahāprasthānika Parva 2–3; Spiti regional tradition) [folk]
- **Cowherd boys sent to beg from the sacrifice** — herding cattle far from Vraja, Krishna's hungry friends are told to ask food of the brāhmaṇas performing a Vedic sacrifice nearby, invoking the names of Krishna and Balarāma _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Balarāma, cowherd boys)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yajna-patni-mokshana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.23.1–3)
- **Dantavakra and Vidūratha slain** — Dantavakra attacks on foot with a mace to avenge Śiśupāla and Śālva and is crushed by Kaumodakī; his brother Vidūratha is beheaded, closing the Jaya–Vijaya cycle _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Dantavakra, Vidūratha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shalva-saubha-vadha.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.78)
- **Death by forest fire** — Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Gāndhārī and Kuntī sit in yogic composure as the conflagration consumes the hermitage; Sañjaya escapes and Nārada brings the news _(actors: Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Gāndhārī, Kuntī, Sañjaya, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/dhritarashtra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Āśramavāsika Parva ~37–39)
- **Destruction of Saubha and death of Śālva** — the Sudarśana cakra cleaves the flying city in two into the ocean and then beheads Śālva, blazing like the sun _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Śālva, Sudarśana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shalva-saubha-vadha.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CE 3.23; Bhāgavata 10.77)
- **Devakī's petition** — hearing that Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma raised Sāndīpani's dead son, the grieving Devakī begs to see once more the six sons Kaṃsa killed _(actors: Devakī, Kṛṣṇa, Balarāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/devaki-six-sons-shadgarbha.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.85.27–33)
- **Dhanvantari born into the Kāśī royal line** — The ocean-born physician-god is reborn as a king of the Kāśī dynasty (son of Dīrghatapas), promulgating Āyurveda in eight divisions _(actors: Dhanvantari, Dīrghatapas)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa IX.17.4–5; Viṣṇu Purāṇa IV.8)
- **Dhṛtarāṣṭra hears the Gītā and viśvarūpa through Sañjaya** — refusing the divine eye for himself, the blind king receives Sañjaya's report of the war, the Bhagavad-Gītā, and the cosmic form _(actors: Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Sañjaya, Vyāsa, Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/dhritarashtra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Bhīṣma Parva; Gītā 1.1, 11)
- **Dhṛtarāṣṭra passed over for the Kuru throne** — the blind eldest prince is set aside and the younger Pāṇḍu is consecrated king of Hāstinapura _(actors: Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Pāṇḍu, Bhīṣma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/dhritarashtra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Dhṛtarāṣṭra restored as Gandharva king** — in Yudhiṣṭhira's tour of the hereafter the blind king is seen glorious among the Gandharvas, the station of his origin _(actors: Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/dhritarashtra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Svargārohaṇa Parva)
- **Digvijaya of Pāṇḍu** — the young king slays Dīrgha of Magadha and reduces the Daśārṇas, Mithilā, Kāśī, Suhma and Puṇḍra, returning to Hāstinapura laden with tribute _(actors: Pāṇḍu, Dīrgha of Magadha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/pandu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Ḍimbhaka drowns in the Yamunā on false news** — hearing "Haṃsa is slain," Ḍimbhaka believes his brother dead and kills himself in the river, untouched by any weapon _(actors: Ḍimbhaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-dimbhaka.md) — (Mahābhārata, Sabhā Parva 2.14)
- **Ḍimbhaka's suicide in the same waters (Harivaṃśa variant)** — pursued by Balarāma and taunted to "ask the river" for his brother, Ḍimbhaka laments and kills himself in the Yamunā _(actors: Ḍimbhaka, Balarāma, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-dimbhaka.md) — (Harivaṃśa vulgate, Bhaviṣya Parva 129)
- **Dragging of Hastināpura** — slighted by the Kurus holding Sāmba captive, Balarāma hooks his plough into the city's ramparts and drags Hastināpura toward the Gaṅgā until the couple is released _(actors: Balarāma, Sāmba, Lakṣmaṇā, Duryodhana, the Kurus)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/balarama.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.68)
- **Dragging of the Yamunā** — visiting Vraja alone for two months, the wine-flushed Balarāma hooks his plough into the Yamunā and drags the river-goddess from her course until she submits _(actors: Balarāma, Yamunā, the gopīs)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/balarama.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.65; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.25)
- **Draupadī and the eight queens** — Draupadī asks each of Kṛṣṇa's aṣṭa-mahiṣī how she was won, gathering the marriage-narratives of Rukmiṇī, Satyabhāmā, Jāmbavatī, Kālindī, Mitravindā, Satyā, Bhadrā and Lakṣmaṇā _(actors: Draupadī, Rukmiṇī, Satyabhāmā, Jāmbavatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kurukshetra-reunion-gopis.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.83)
- **Durvāsas curses Bhānumatī in the Raivata garden** — the offended sage dooms Bhānu's daughter to be carried off by an enemy, then softens the curse to promise her purity and a good husband _(actors: Durvāsas, Bhānumatī, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nikumbha-bhanumati-shatpura.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva ch. 91, Dutt)
- **Dushyanta weds Śakuntalā near Kaṇva's āśrama at Nandaprayag** — Garhwal tradition localizes the gāndharva marriage that produced Bharata to this valley [folk] [regional] _(actors: Dushyanta, Śakuntalā, Kaṇva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva base; Wikipedia, Panch Prayag)
- **Dvārakā sinks and Vajra inherits the remnant** — After Kṛṣṇa's departure by the hunter Jarā's arrow and Balarāma's yogic death, the sea swallows Dvārakā; Arjuna evacuates the survivors and Kṛṣṇa's great-grandson Vajra is installed as the last Yādava king _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Balarāma, Jarā, Arjuna, Vajra)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/yadava-vamsha.md) — (Mahābhārata Mausala Parva 4-8; Bhāgavata XI.30-31; Viṣṇu Purāṇa V.38)
- **Dvivida-vadha** — the rogue mountain-hurling ape Dvivida, avenging Narakāsura by ravaging Ānarta, is battered to death by Balarāma on Raivataka hill _(actors: Balarāma, Dvivida)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/balarama.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.67)
- **Forest retirement of the old king** — fifteen years after the war, Dhṛtarāṣṭra retires with Gāndhārī, Kuntī, Vidura and Sañjaya to a Gaṅgā-side hermitage _(actors: Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Gāndhārī, Kuntī, Vidura, Sañjaya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/dhritarashtra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Āśramavāsika Parva)
- **Fourfold division of Vajranābha's kingdom** — Kṛṣṇa and Indra share the demon realm among Vijaya son of Jayanta and the newborn sons of Pradyumna, Sāmba and Gada, and the Yādavas return to Dwārakā _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Indra, Vijaya, Candraprabha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pradyumna-prabhavati-vajranabha.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva 98, Dutt)
- **Funeral rites at Hāstinapura** — the Śataśṛṅga ascetics deliver Kuntī, the five boys and the remains of Pāṇḍu and Mādrī to the capital, where Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Bhīṣma and Vidura perform the śrāddha _(actors: Kuntī, the Pāṇḍavas, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Bhīṣma, Vidura)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/pandu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Gāndharva marriage of Pradyumna and Prabhāvatī** — entering her apartments as a black bee in a garland, Pradyumna weds Prabhāvatī before a self-blazing fire; Gada and Sāmba secretly wed her cousins Candrāvatī and Guṇavatī _(actors: Pradyumna, Prabhāvatī, Gada, Sāmba)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pradyumna-prabhavati-vajranabha.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva 95, Dutt)
- **Gonanda I of Kashmir falls to Balarāma** — The Kashmir king Gonanda I, kinsman-ally of Jarāsandha, joins the siege of Mathurā and is slain in battle by Balarāma _(actors: Gonanda I, Jarāsandha, Balarāma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/nilamata-purana.md) — (Nīlamata Purāṇa; Rājataraṅgiṇī 1.57–60)
- **Gopīs of Vraja canonized as the paradigm of parā bhakti** — their prema for Kṛṣṇa is named the living exemplar of supreme love, defended as no mere paramour's passion _(actors: gopīs of Vraja, Kṛṣṇa, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhakti-sutras-narada-shandilya.md) — (Nārada Bhakti Sūtra, 21–24)
- **Govardhan Annakūṭa — the mountain of food** — Kṛṣṇa redirects Indra's sacrifice to Govardhana Hill heaped with food and lifts the hill to shelter Vraja from Indra's flood, founding the annakūṭa / chappan-bhoga offering _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, the cowherds, Indra)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/anna-naivedya-and-prasada.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.24–25)
- **Haṃsa drowns after him; Jarāsandha turns home "with an empty heart"** — the real Haṃsa, learning of Ḍimbhaka's suicide, enters the Yamunā, and the bereft Jarāsandha abandons his Mathurā campaign _(actors: Haṃsa, Jarāsandha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-dimbhaka.md) — (Mahābhārata, Sabhā Parva 2.14)
- **Hanumān humbles Bhīma on the Gandhamādana path** — the surviving vānara chirañjīvi tests his Vāyu-born brother with his immovable tail _(actors: Hanumān, Bhīma)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/vanaras-and-rikshas.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva)
- **Installation of Jñāna Sarasvatī at Vyāsapurī** — the sand-mūrtis are consecrated with Sarasvatī as presiding goddess, founding the Basara Śākta Tridevī kṣetra _(actors: Vyāsa, Sarasvatī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/basara-gnana-saraswati-vyasa.md) — (Basara sthala-tradition)
- **Jāmbavān fights Kṛṣṇa over the Syamantaka jewel** — after a cave-battle of many days the bear-king recognizes Rāma returned, surrenders the gem and gives Jāmbavatī in marriage _(actors: Jāmbavān, Kṛṣṇa, Jāmbavatī)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/vanaras-and-rikshas.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.56)
- **Jantu immolated over the mothers' resistance** — the queens seize and drag Jantu away weeping, but the priest wrests him free, slays him, offers his fat into the fire, and the swooning mothers are made to breathe the smoke _(actors: the purohita, Jantu, the hundred queens)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/jantu-somaka.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CXXVII–CXXVIII)
- **Kaṃsa slays the six infants** — fulfilling the curse, the tyrant Kaṃsa dashes Devakī's first six newborns to death, the atrocity the Ṣaḍgarbha arc later resolves _(actors: Kaṃsa, Devakī, the six infants)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/devaki-six-sons-shadgarbha.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.1; 10.85.50)
- **Kaṃsa usurps Mathurā and imprisons Ugrasena** — The Andhaka prince Kaṃsa jails his father King Ugrasena and, warned that Devakī's eighth son will kill him, imprisons Vasudeva and Devakī and slays their first six infants _(actors: Kaṃsa, Ugrasena, Vasudeva, Devakī)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/yadava-vamsha.md) — (Bhāgavata X.1-4; Harivaṃśa Viṣṇu Parva; Viṣṇu Purāṇa V.1-4)
- **Kaṇva narrates the Mātali-upākhyāna** — during Kṛṣṇa's failed peace-embassy the sage Kaṇva tells Duryodhana the tale of Mātali and Garuḍa as a warning against pride and irreconcilable enmity _(actors: Kaṇva, Duryodhana, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matali-sumukha-naga-bridegroom.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva 5.95)
- **Karṇa and Draupadī charter-variants of Chhath** — folk aetiologies place the first riverside sun-vrata to Sūrya and Chhathī Maiyā with Sūrya's son Karṇa, or with Draupadī and the Pāṇḍavas seeking their kingdom [folk] _(actors: Karṇa, Draupadī, Sūrya, Chhathī Maiyā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevata-shashthi-cross-links.md) — (Chhath folk tradition, via regional-temple-festivals)
- **Karṇa spares Nakula** — Defeating Nakula in single combat on Day 17, Karṇa honours his promise to Kuntī and dismisses him alive with a taunt and a touch of the bow _(actors: Karṇa, Nakula, Kuntī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/nakula-sahadeva.md) — (Mahabharata, Karna Parva)
- **Karṇa's tapas at Karnaprayag** — Karṇa worships his father Sūrya at the Piṇḍar–Alaknandā confluence and obtains the golden kavacha-kuṇḍala; Garhwal localization of the epic episode [regional] _(actors: Karṇa, Sūrya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva base; Wikipedia, Panch Prayag)
- **Kashmir sits out the Kurukṣetra war** — Because the infant Gonanda II is too young to be courted by either side, no king of Kashmir is summoned to the Bhārata war — the question that frames the whole Nīlamata _(actors: Gonanda II, the Kurus, the Pāṇḍavas)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/nilamata-purana.md) — (Nīlamata Purāṇa, opening; Rājataraṅgiṇī 1.82)
- **Kāśī withholds alms from Vyāsa** — to test the sage, Śiva has Pārvatī see that no household in the city gives Vyāsa or his disciples any alms _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Vyāsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyasa-banished-from-kashi.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāśī-khaṇḍa, Uttarārdha 96)
- **King Nanda's yajña at Nandaprayag** — a King Nanda, identified by tradition with Krishna's Yādava foster-father, performs a great sacrifice at the Nandākinī confluence, giving the prayāga its name [regional] _(actors: Nanda)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (Wikipedia, Panch Prayag)
- **King Nṛga freed from chameleon-form** — Krishna lifts a giant chameleon from a dry well and it resumes the form of King Nṛga, cursed for misgiving a brāhmaṇa's cow; released, he ascends _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Nṛga, the Yādava boys)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/krishna-lila.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.64)
- **Krishna blesses their love and sends them home** — receiving their offering in the grove, Krishna praises the wives' devotion as life's highest fruit but bids them return to complete their husbands' rite, teaching that hearing and remembering him suffice without bodily nearness _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, yajña-patnīs)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yajna-patni-mokshana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.23.24–34)
- **Krishna descends to Varuṇa-loka** — hearing the cowherds cry "Kṛṣṇa! Rāma!", Krishna understands his father has been taken and goes down into the watery abode of Varuṇa to reclaim him _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, cowherds, Nanda)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nanda-varuna-vaikuntha-darshana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.28.3–4)
- **Krishna performs Karṇa's last rites near Karnaprayag** — a local variant extends the site's claim from Karṇa's penance to his post-Kurukṣetra obsequies [folk] [disputed] _(actors: Krishna, Karṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (local tradition, per Wikipedia, Panch Prayag)
- **Krishna redirects the boys to the wives** — unruffled by the refusal, Krishna sends the boys to the yajña-patnīs, the sacrificers' devoted wives, announcing that he and Balarāma have come and are hungry _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, cowherd boys, yajña-patnīs)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yajna-patni-mokshana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.23.13–14)
- **Kṛṣṇa becomes Raṇacchoḍa** — quitting Mathurā's battlefield for the western sea, Kṛṣṇa earns the epithet *Raṇchhoḍa* borne by the Dvārakā image later enshrined at Ḍākor _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Jarāsandha, Kālayavana)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dakor-ranchhodrai-bodana.md) — (Bhāgavata 10.50–52)
- **Kṛṣṇa beheads Nikumbha with the discus** — discerning the true dānava amid his phantom doubles, Kṛṣṇa cuts off Nikumbha's head and the demon falls like an uprooted tree _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Nikumbha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nikumbha-bhanumati-shatpura.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva ch. 91, Dutt)
- **Kṛṣṇa bilocates for Śrutadeva and Bahulāśva** — Visiting Mithilā, Krishna simultaneously guests with the poor brāhmaṇa Śrutadeva and King Bahulāśva, honoring the equal devotion of pauper and monarch _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Śrutadeva, Bahulāśva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/krishna-lila.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.86)
- **Kṛṣṇa cites the drownings in the Rājasūya counsel** — with Haṃsa, Ḍimbhaka, and Kaṃsa dead, Kṛṣṇa tells Yudhiṣṭhira that Jarāsandha is stripped of his supports and can now be slain, but only in single combat by Bhīma _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Yudhiṣṭhira, Bhīma, Jarāsandha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-dimbhaka.md) — (Mahābhārata, Sabhā Parva 2.13–14)
- **Kṛṣṇa declares Mārgaśīrṣa his vibhūti** — "Of months I am Mārgaśīrṣa," spoken in the vibhūti-yoga at Kurukṣetra, the epic's own month-consecration _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/masa-mahatmyas-kartika-magha-vaishakha.md) — (Bhagavad-gītā 10.35)
- **Kṛṣṇa destroys Śālva's Saubha** — Śiśupāla's ally Śālva besieges Dwārakā with the Śiva-granted aerial fortress Saubha; Krishna shatters the machine and beheads Śālva _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Śālva, Pradyumna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/krishna-lila.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.76–77)
- **Kṛṣṇa distinguishes sannyāsa from tyāga** — at Kurukṣetra Kṛṣṇa rules that sannyāsa is giving up desire-driven rites while tyāga is giving up the fruits of all action _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sannyasa-and-the-renunciate-tradition.md) — (Bhagavad Gītā, 18.1–12)
- **Kṛṣṇa follows Rukmiṇī to Diṇḍīrvan** — in the Dvārakā-frame variant, Kṛṣṇa comes from Dvārakā seeking the estranged Rukmiṇī sulking in the Diṇḍīravana grove near Pandharpur, rooting the Deccan kṣetra in his Dvāpara life _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Rukmiṇī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pandharpur-vitthala-wari.md) — (Pāṇḍuraṅga-māhātmya, Skanda/Padma recensions) [regional]
- **Kṛṣṇa kicks Haṃsa into the Kāliya pool (Harivaṃśa variant)** — routed by the Vaiṣṇava weapon, Haṃsa flees into the Yamunā abyss where Kāliya once lived and Kṛṣṇa's kick sends him to the serpent-deeps, never to rise _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Haṃsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-dimbhaka.md) — (Harivaṃśa vulgate, Bhaviṣya Parva 128)
- **Kṛṣṇa narrates the Ekādaśī-māhātmyas to Yudhiṣṭhira** — the master frame of the year-cycle: Yudhiṣṭhira asks the name, method, and fruit of each month's Ekādaśī and Kṛṣṇa answers with its kathā _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ekadashi-mahatmyas-catalog.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa 39–63) [late text]
- **Kṛṣṇa prescribes the Ananta vrata to Yudhiṣṭhira** — after the dice-game dispossession, Kṛṣṇa teaches the fourteen-knot Ananta Chaturdaśī vow as the means to recover the kingdom _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vrata-kathas-catalog.md) — (Bhaviṣyottara Purāṇa [attrib.], Ananta-vrata-kathā) [late text]
- **Kṛṣṇa seeks a son at Upamanyu's hermitage** — Grieving Jāmbavatī's sonlessness, Kṛṣṇa travels to the Himālayan hermitage of the ageless sage Upamanyu to win a son by tapas to Śiva _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Jāmbavatī, Upamanyu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/upamanyu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva XIV; Śiva Purāṇa, Umā-saṃhitā 1)
- **Kṛṣṇa slays Dāmodara and crowns the widow Yaśovatī** — Gonanda's avenging son Dāmodara is killed by Kṛṣṇa's discus at a Gāndhāra svayaṃvara, and Kṛṣṇa has the pregnant queen Yaśovatī consecrated because Kashmir is Pārvatī herself _(actors: Dāmodara I, Kṛṣṇa, Yaśovatī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/nilamata-purana.md) — (Nīlamata Purāṇa; Rājataraṅgiṇī 1.62–72)
- **Kṛṣṇa slays Kaṃsa and restores Ugrasena** — Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma kill the usurper at Mathurā and reinstall Ugrasena as king, keeping Yadu's curse — no Yādava takes the throne himself _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Balarāma, Kaṃsa, Ugrasena)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/yadava-vamsha.md) — (Bhāgavata X.44-45; Viṣṇu Purāṇa V.20-21; Harivaṃśa)
- **Kṛṣṇa teaches the jaṅgama-tīrtha doctrine to Akrūra** — Kṛṣṇa declares that saints are 'moving tīrthas' who purify the tīrthas themselves, since the Lord dwells in their hearts _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Akrūra)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tirtha-and-tirtha-yatra.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.48.31)
- **Kṛṣṇa worships his own image at Dvārakā and charges Uddhava** — Kṛṣṇa keeps the mūrti in his Dvārakā shrine and, before departing, tells Uddhava it will float free of the coming flood for Bṛhaspati to secure _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Uddhava, Bṛhaspati)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/guruvayur-krishna-kshetra.md) — (Guruvāyupura-māhātmya)
- **Kṛṣṇa's confession at Kāmyaka** — visiting the exiled Pāṇḍavas, Kṛṣṇa narrates the whole Saubha war in the first person to explain his absence from the dice-game _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Yudhiṣṭhira, the Pāṇḍavas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shalva-saubha-vadha.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CE 3.13–15)
- **Kṛṣṇa's rāsa-līlā becomes the archetype of danced worship** — the Vṛndāvana rāsa is the charter-myth later embodied in Manipuri Rāsa-līlā and the rāsa folk-dances _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Rādhā, the gopīs)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/classical-dance-forms-and-ritual-theatre.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, X.29–33)
- **Kṛṣṇa's Rukmiṇī-marriage rejoins Jyāmagha's two branches** — Kṛṣṇa (descended from Vidarbha's son Kratha via Sātvata) carries off Rukmiṇī of the Vidarbha house, marrying back within Jyāmagha's own posterity _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Rukmiṇī, Bhīṣmaka)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/jyamagha.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa IV.12–13, V.26; Bhāgavata Purāṇa IX.24, X.52–54)
- **Kṛṣṇa's teaching to the gopīs** — in private he consoles the gopīs with jñāna, declaring himself the one all-pervading Self so that their separation is only apparent and their love-longing is itself yoga _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, the gopīs)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kurukshetra-reunion-gopis.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.82.44–45)
- **Kṛṣṇa's war with Indra (Indra-yuddha)** — Indra pursues with the Ādityas, Maruts and Vasus and hurls the Vajra, but the deva-rāja is worsted by his own supreme source, Viṣṇu incarnate on Garuḍa _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Indra, Garuḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/parijata-harana.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva 76; Bhāgavata X.59)
- **Kuntī's counter-precedent** — refusing to be sent to a stranger's bed, Kuntī answers Pāṇḍu with the ancient tale of Vyuṣitāśva and asks him instead to beget children by his own ascetic power _(actors: Kuntī, Pāṇḍu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyushitashva-bhadra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva; Ganguli §CXXI)
- **Lomaśa leads the Pāṇḍavas' tīrtha-yātrā** — the exiled Pāṇḍavas walk the all-India ford-circuit under the sage Lomaśa, dramatizing pilgrimage as the poor person's yajña _(actors: Lomaśa, Yudhiṣṭhira, the Pāṇḍavas, Dhaumya)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tirtha-and-tirtha-yatra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva 89–156)
- **Mādrī's anumaraṇa on Pāṇḍu's pyre** — The junior queen wins the contest of devotion against Kuntī and ascends her husband's funeral pyre by her own insistent choice _(actors: Mādrī, Kuntī, Pāṇḍu)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sati-jauhar-and-the-immolation-rites.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Many rasas at Kaṃsa's arena** — Kṛṣṇa appears differently to each beholder (thunderbolt to wrestlers, death to Kaṃsa, child to the elders), the proof-text for devotion refracted through bhāva _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Kaṃsa, the wrestlers, the people of Mathurā)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/bhakti-rasa-and-the-bhavas.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.43.17)
- **Mokṣadā Ekādaśī as Gītā Jayantī** — tradition fixes the speaking of the Bhagavad-gītā at Kurukṣetra to the Mārgaśīrṣa śukla Ekādaśī, doubling Mokṣadā as Gītā Jayantī _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ekadashi-mahatmyas-catalog.md) — (traditional identification; Padma Purāṇa Ekādaśī cycle) [late text]
- **Muṣṭika slain in Kaṃsa's arena** — Balarāma crushes the champion wrestler Muṣṭika and then Kaṃsa's eight avenging brothers at Mathurā _(actors: Balarāma, Muṣṭika, Kṛṣṇa, Kaṃsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/balarama.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.44)
- **Nakula questions Bhīṣma on escaping Yama** — on the field at Kurukṣetra the youngest Pāṇḍava asks his dying grandsire how a soul is freed from death's authority, and Bhīṣma repeats Yama's secret charge _(actors: Nakula, Bhīṣma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yama-gita.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa III.7)
- **Nakula slays the sons of Karṇa** — In the Day 18 fighting Nakula kills Citrasena, Satyasena, and Suṣeṇa, three sons of Karṇa _(actors: Nakula, Citrasena, Satyasena, Suṣeṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/nakula-sahadeva.md) — (Mahabharata, Shalya Parva)
- **Nakula's western digvijaya** — For Yudhiṣṭhira's Rājasūya, Nakula subdues Rohītaka, the Śibis and the western peoples and receives the affectionate submission of his uncle Śalya of Madra, returning with tribute borne by ten thousand camels _(actors: Nakula, Śalya, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/nakula-sahadeva.md) — (Mahabharata, Sabha Parva)
- **Nanda seized by Varuṇa's servant** — bathing in the Yamunā at a forbidden pre-dawn hour on the Dvādaśī after his Ekādaśī fast, Nanda is carried off to the undersea court of the water-lord by a demoniac servant of Varuṇa _(actors: Nanda, Varuṇa's servant, Varuṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nanda-varuna-vaikuntha-darshana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.28.1–2)
- **Nanda's report and the cowherds' silent wish** — Nanda describes the honour Varuṇa paid to Krishna, and the cowherds inwardly wonder whether their boy can grant them his own supreme abode _(actors: Nanda, cowherds, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nanda-varuna-vaikuntha-darshana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.28.11–12)
- **Nārada retells the Nimi–Yogendra dialogue to Vasudeva** — at Dvārakā on the eve of Kṛṣṇa's departure, Vasudeva asks about bhāgavata-dharma and Nārada answers with the ancient Videha saṃvāda _(actors: Nārada, Vasudeva, Devakī, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nimi-nava-yogendra-samvada.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 11.2; 11.5.41–52)
- **Nārada's Pārijāta blossom** — Nārada brings a single Pārijāta flower to Kṛṣṇa's court and Kṛṣṇa gives it to Rukmiṇī, then goads Satyabhāmā that the whole tree stands in Indra's heaven, igniting the co-wife rivalry _(actors: Nārada, Kṛṣṇa, Rukmiṇī, Satyabhāmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/parijata-harana.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva 69; Bhāgavata X.59)
- **Navaguñjara theophany to Arjuna** — a composite beast of nine animals appears to Arjuna during his penance on Maṇibhadra hill; recognizing that no natural creature could exist in such a form, he lowers the Gāṇḍīva and worships it as Viṣṇu/Kṛṣṇa _(actors: Arjuna, Viṣṇu/Kṛṣṇa (as Navaguñjara))_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/mythic-beasts-and-composites.md) — (Sāraḷā Dāsa, Oḍiā Mahābhārata [folk/regional])
- **Nikumbha abducts Bhānumatī** — while the Yādava warriors sport at the festival, the illusion-wielding dānava snatches Bhānu's daughter from the palace garden and flies to Ṣaṭpura _(actors: Nikumbha, Bhānumatī, Bhānu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nikumbha-bhanumati-shatpura.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva ch. 91, Dutt)
- **Niyoga birth of Dhṛtarāṣṭra** — Ambikā closes her eyes before Vyāsa during the levirate rite and her son is born blind _(actors: Vyāsa, Ambikā, Satyavatī, Bhīṣma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/dhritarashtra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Niyoga birth of Pāṇḍu** — Ambālikā turns pale before the sage Vyāsa during the levirate rite, so her son is born pale and named Pāṇḍu _(actors: Vyāsa, Ambālikā, Satyavatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/pandu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Outrage at Puṣkara** — Haṃsa and Ḍimbhaka wreck the ascetics' hermitage and defile it with meat-cooking, and Durvāsas leads five thousand sages to Dvārakā to lay the sacrilege before Kṛṣṇa _(actors: Haṃsa, Ḍimbhaka, Durvāsas, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-dimbhaka.md) — (Harivaṃśa vulgate, Bhaviṣya Parva 107–110)
- **Pāṇḍu crowned past his blind elder brother** — Dhṛtarāṣṭra is set aside for blindness and Pāṇḍu is consecrated king of Hāstinapura, seeding the Kaurava grievance _(actors: Pāṇḍu, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Bhīṣma, Vidura)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/pandu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Pāṇḍu seen among the risen dead** — Vyāsa raises the departed to sight for one night on the Gaṅgā, and Pāṇḍu appears before his sons and widows _(actors: Vyāsa, Pāṇḍu, Kuntī, the Pāṇḍavas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/pandu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Āśramavāsika Parva)
- **Pāṇḍu's plea for offspring** — barred from fatherhood by the Kindama curse, Pāṇḍu invokes the ancient liberty of women and Śvetaketu's reform to urge Kuntī to raise up heirs for his line _(actors: Pāṇḍu, Kuntī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyushitashva-bhadra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva; Ganguli §CXX)
- **Paraśurāma tells the Dambhodbhava tale in the Kaurava court** — during Kṛṣṇa's peace embassy, Rāma Jāmadagnya narrates the world-conqueror's humbling as a warning against Duryodhana's pride _(actors: Paraśurāma, Duryodhana, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dambhodbhava-nara-narayana.md) — (Mahābhārata CE 5.94, Udyoga Parva, Bhagavad-yāna)
- **Partition of the Kuru kingdom** — Dhṛtarāṣṭra grants the Pāṇḍavas the wilderness of Khāṇḍavaprastha, which they raise into Indraprastha _(actors: Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Yudhiṣṭhira, Duryodhana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/dhritarashtra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Patraṃ puṣpaṃ phalaṃ toyam — the offering charter** — Kṛṣṇa declares he accepts a mere leaf, flower, fruit or water given with devotion, and that eaters of the remnant of sacrifice are freed from sin, converting every meal into naivedya and prasāda _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/anna-naivedya-and-prasada.md) — (Bhagavad Gītā 9.26; 3.13)
- **Pauṇḍraka joins the anti-Kṛṣṇa axis** — Pauṇḍraka-Vāsudeva, lord of the Puṇḍras/Vaṅgas/Kirātas, is named among Jarāsandha's allied kings and later escorts Śiśupāla to Rukmiṇī's wedding _(actors: Pauṇḍraka, Jarāsandha, Śiśupāla)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/paundraka-the-false-vasudeva.md) — (Mahābhārata, Sabhā Parva; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.53)
- **Pauṇḍraka slain and Kāśī burned** — Krishna beheads the imposter 'Vāsudeva' Pauṇḍraka and the Kāśī king; when Sudakṣiṇa's abhicāra fire-demon attacks Dwārakā, the Sudarśana cakra drives it back and incinerates Kāśī _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Pauṇḍraka, the Kāśī king, Sudakṣiṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/krishna-lila.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.66)
- **Pauṇḍraka-vadha at Kāśī** — Kṛṣṇa breaks the five akṣauhiṇīs of the impostor and his ally, beheads Pauṇḍraka with the Sudarśana cakra, and hurls the Kāśī king's severed head into his own city _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Pauṇḍraka, the Kāśī king)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/paundraka-the-false-vasudeva.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.66; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.34)
- **Poigai's cosmic lamp** — the first Āḻvār kindles a lamp of earth-vessel, ocean-ghee and sun-flame while Bhūtam lights one of love and Pey sees Śrī before Viṣṇu, composing the earliest 300 verses of the Prabandham _(actors: Poigai, Bhūtam, Pey Āḻvār, Lakṣmī, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvar-hagiographies-guruparampara.md) — (Guruparampara Prabhāvam 6000-paḍi)
- **Pradyumna's stand and the charioteer's dharma** — Pradyumna champions the city's defense, is struck senseless, rebukes the charioteer who saved him, then is stopped from killing Śālva, whose death is fated for Kṛṣṇa _(actors: Pradyumna, Śālva, son of Dāruka, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shalva-saubha-vadha.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CE 3.17–21)
- **Pūjanī blinds Brahmadatta's prince** — the palace bird Pūjanī, whose chick the infant prince crushed, tears out his eyes in revenge and refuses the king's peace, declaring that kindled enmity never dies _(actors: Pūjanī, King Brahmadatta of Kāmpilya, the prince)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shanti-parva-minor-upakhyanas.md) — (MBh, Śānti Parva 12.137)
- **Pulastya recites the tīrtha catalogue to Bhīṣma** — the sage Pulastya teaches young Bhīṣma the roster of fords and their phala, with the warning that only the self-controlled gain the fruit _(actors: Pulastya, Bhīṣma)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tirtha-and-tirtha-yatra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva 82–85)
- **Rādhā's birth at Barsānā/Rāval** — Rādhā is born (or found on a lotus) as daughter of King Vṛṣabhānu and Kīrtidā in Braj _(actors: Rādhā, Vṛṣabhānu, Kīrtidā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/radha.md) — (Padma Purāṇa late portions; Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa) [late text]
- **Rādhā's māna–separation–reunion arc (Gīta-Govinda narrative)** — Jayadeva's lyric recentres Kṛṣṇa's Vṛndāvana rāsa on the single beloved Rādhā, through jealous pride (yāhi mādhava), Kṛṣṇa's plea to place her foot on his head (dehi pada-pallavam), and reunion where God serves the beloved (kuru yadunandana) [late text] _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Rādhā, the sakhī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jayadeva-and-the-gita-govinda.md) — (Gīta-Govinda, sargas 1–12)
- **Rantideva among the sixteen dead kings** — recited amid the consolation for Abhimanyu, Vyāsa names Rantideva a cakravartin at whose table two thousand cattle were dressed daily, yet who died — teaching the vanity of grief _(actors: Vyāsa, Yudhiṣṭhira, Rantideva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rantideva.md) — (Mahābhārata, Droṇa Parva ~67; Śānti Parva ~29)
- **Rāsa-līlā as the paradigm of madhurya-rasa** — the gopīs' autumn-night dance with Kṛṣṇa becomes the archetypal event of conjugal devotional rasa, with Rādhā's mahābhāva its summit _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Rādhā, the gopīs)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/bhakti-rasa-and-the-bhavas.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.29–33)
- **Robbers murder the golden boy for an empty hoard** — believing Suvarṇaṣṭhīvin's body full of gold, robbers slay and cut him open, find nothing, and perish, leaving Sṛñjaya bereft of both son and gold at a stroke _(actors: the robbers, Suvarṇaṣṭhīvin, Sṛñjaya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/suvarnashthivin-srinjaya-narada-parvata.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva; Ganguli XXXI)
- **Sahadeva offers the arghya to Kṛṣṇa** — At the Rājasūya Sahadeva, at Bhīṣma's direction, presents the first-honour offering to Kṛṣṇa, triggering Śiśupāla's fatal outburst _(actors: Sahadeva, Kṛṣṇa, Bhīṣma, Śiśupāla)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/nakula-sahadeva.md) — (Mahabharata, Sabha Parva)
- **Sahadeva slays Ulūka and Śakuni** — On the afternoon of Day 18 Sahadeva kills Śakuni's son Ulūka and then beheads Śakuni with a broad-headed arrow, reminding him of the dice and fulfilling his dice-hall vow _(actors: Sahadeva, Śakuni, Ulūka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/nakula-sahadeva.md) — (Mahabharata, Shalya Parva)
- **Sahadeva's southern digvijaya and the fire at Māhiṣmatī** — Sahadeva conquers the south for the Rājasūya, propitiating Agni who defends king Nīla's city, and levies tribute from the Pāṇḍyas, Dravidas, and by envoys from Vibhīṣaṇa of Laṅkā _(actors: Sahadeva, Nīla, Agni, Vibhīṣaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/nakula-sahadeva.md) — (Mahabharata, Sabha Parva)
- **Śakuni's bone-dice vengeance** — the Kauravas starve Śakuni's Gāndhāra kin to death; dice carved from his father's bones drive his covert engineering of the Kauravas' ruin [regional] _(actors: Śakuni, Duryodhana, the Kauravas)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/odia-literature-sarala-and-panchasakha.md) — (Sāraḷā Dāsa, Sāraḷā Mahābhārata)
- **Śālva besieges Dvārakā** — avenging Śiśupāla's death at the Rājasūya, Śālva assaults Dvārakā from the airborne Saubha while Kṛṣṇa is away at Indraprastha _(actors: Śālva, Ugrasena, the Vṛṣṇis)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shalva-saubha-vadha.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CE 3.15–16)
- **Śālva wins the Śiva-boon and the Saubha** — after a year-long vow to Paśupati, Śālva receives the Maya-built flying iron city Saubha, unassailable by devas, asuras or men _(actors: Śālva, Śiva, Maya Dānava)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shalva-saubha-vadha.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 10.76)
- **Śalya marches to join the Pāṇḍavas** — Śalya sets out from Madra with his army intending to fight for his sister's sons, the Pāṇḍavas _(actors: Śalya, the Pāṇḍava host)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/shalya.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva (Sainyodyoga))
- **Arjuna and the Gandharva — the 'Tāpatya' frame** — Arjuna defeats the gandharva Aṅgāraparṇa/Citraratha on the Gaṅgā, is hailed as 'Tāpatya,' and asks why — prompting the Saṃvaraṇa–Tapatī tale _(actors: Arjuna, Aṅgāraparṇa/Citraratha)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/samvarana-tapati.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva, Caitraratha-parvan ~169)
- **Arjuna Conceived by Indra** — Kunti uses the Durvasa boon-mantra to invoke Indra, who begets Arjuna, the third Pandava, in the Shatashringa hermitage _(actors: Kunti, Indra, Arjuna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/arjuna.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi Parva ~123)
- **Arjuna reaches Maṇipura on his tīrtha-yātrā** — During the twelfth year of his exile-pilgrimage, Arjuna arrives at the kingdom of Maṇipura and beholds the princess Citrāṅgadā _(actors: Arjuna, Citrāṅgadā, Citravāhana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/chitrangada.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva ~206–207)
- **Arjuna sees Subhadrā at Dvārakā** — During his tīrtha-pilgrimage Arjuna comes to Dvārakā/Raivataka and is smitten by Subhadrā at the Vṛṣṇi festival _(actors: Arjuna, Subhadrā, Kṛṣṇa, Vṛṣṇi Yādavas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/subhadra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva (Subhadrā-haraṇa Parva))
- **Bāṇa's boon of war** — The thousand-armed asura Bāṇāsura, vain of his arms, begs Śiva for a worthy battle; Śiva foretells his humbling when his banner-staff breaks _(actors: Bāṇāsura, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/banasura-usha-aniruddha.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.62; Harivaṃśa Viṣṇu Parva; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5)
- **Bṛhadaśva Begins the Tale of Nala** — In exile Yudhiṣṭhira asks if any king suffered worse than he; the sage Bṛhadaśva answers with Nala's story and teaches him the secret of dice _(actors: Yudhiṣṭhira, Bṛhadaśva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nala-damayanti.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva 50, 78)
- **Bṛhadaśva narrates Nala's tale to Yudhiṣṭhira** — Sage Bṛhadaśva consoles the exiled Yudhiṣṭhira with the Nala story and teaches him the akṣa-hṛdaya (secret of dice) _(actors: Bṛhadaśva, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nala-damayanti.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva 50, 78)
- **Bhima Born of Vayu** — Kunti uses the Durvasa-mantra to summon Vayu, who begets Bhima, the second Pandava endowed with superhuman strength _(actors: Kunti, Vayu, Bhima)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/bhima.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi Parva ~123)
- **Bhishma's terrible vow** — Devavrata renounces the throne and vows lifelong celibacy so Satyavati's line may inherit, earning the name Bhishma and the boon of death-at-will _(actors: Devavrata/Bhishma, Shantanu, Satyavati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/01-adi-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Adi Parva)
- **Bhīma Catches the Bull-Form Shiva at Guptakāśī** — Fleeing the Pandavas seeking expiation for the Kurukshetra slaughter, Shiva as a bull is recognized by Bhima at Guptakashi; seized by the tail, the bull dives into the earth to re-emerge in five parts across Garhwal _(actors: Bhima, Pandavas, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-kedar-pancha-badri.md) — (Skanda Purana (Kedara-khanda); Mahabharata tradition)
- **Bhīṣma Delivers the Mokṣadharma** — From his bed of arrows the dying Bhīṣma teaches Yudhiṣṭhira the dharma of liberation, the largest philosophical block of the epic _(actors: Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mokshadharma-dialogues.md) — (Mahabharata Shanti Parva (Mokshadharma))
- **Bhīṣma Recites the Nārāyaṇīya** — In the aftermath of Kurukṣetra, the dying Bhīṣma teaches Yudhiṣṭhira the Nārāyaṇīya, systematizing Kṛṣṇa-Vāsudeva as supreme Brahman and Pāñcarātra theology _(actors: Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narayaniya.md) — (Mahabharata Shanti Parva (Mokshadharma) 321-339)
- **Birth and primacy of Citrāṅgada** — Citrāṅgada is born as the firstborn son of Śāntanu and Satyavatī, becoming eldest heir after Bhīṣma's renunciation of the throne _(actors: Citrāṅgada, Śāntanu, Satyavatī, Bhīṣma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/vichitravirya-and-chitrangada.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva ~101; Viṣṇu Purāṇa IV.20)
- **Birth as an aṃśa of Dvāpara** — Śakuni, son of Subala and brother of Gāndhārī, is born of a portion of the age-spirit Dvāpara, the embodiment of the gambling-die _(actors: Śakuni, Subala, Gāndhārī, Dvāpara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/shakuni.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva (Aṃśāvataraṇa ~1.61))
- **Birth from the fish** — Born of King Uparicara Vasu's seed and the cursed apsarā-fish Adrikā, the girl twin is raised by the fisher-chief and carries a fish-smell (Matsyagandhā) _(actors: Uparicara Vasu, Adrikā, the fisher-chief (Dāśa), Satyavatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/satyavati.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva ~57)
- **Birth of Babhruvahana to Arjuna and Chitrangada** — Babhruvahana is born to Chitrangada of Manipura during Arjuna's tirtha-exile, bound by pact to remain as matrilineal heir of Manipura _(actors: Babhruvāhana, Arjuna, Citrāṅgadā, Citravāhana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/babhruvahana.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva ~207–208)
- **Birth of Barbarīka** — Barbarīka is born to Ghaṭotkaca and Maurvī/Kāmakaṇṭakaṭā, grandson of Bhīma, carrying both rākṣasa and Pāṇḍava blood _(actors: Barbarīka, Ghaṭotkaca, Maurvī/Kāmakaṇṭakaṭā, Bhīma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/barbarika-khatu-shyam.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa (Kāmārika Khaṇḍa); folk Mahābhārata traditions)
- **Birth of Duhshasana from the flesh-ball** — Duhshasana is born as the second of the hundred Kauravas, the second piece to ripen from Gandhari's divided ball of flesh _(actors: Duḥśāsana, Gāndhārī, Vyāsa, Duryodhana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/dushasana.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva ~107; Aṃśāvataraṇa ~61)
- **Birth of Irāvān to Arjuna and Ulūpī** — During his exile Arjuna unites with the Nāga princess Ulūpī (daughter of Kauravya) beneath the Gaṅgā, begetting Irāvān, who is reared in the serpent-realm _(actors: Arjuna, Ulūpī, Kauravya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/aravan-iravan.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva ~206)
- **Birth of Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana on the island** — Parāśara fathers Vyāsa on the fisher-maiden Satyavatī on a Yamunā island; he is born dark and island-born, matures instantly, and vows to come whenever remembered _(actors: Parāśara, Satyavatī (Matsyagandhā), Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana Vyāsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyasa.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 3.3; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 1.4)
- **Birth of Kripa and Kripi from Sharadvat's seed** — Sage Sharadvat's seed falls on shara-grass at the sight of the apsara Janapadi and splits into twin children, Kripa and Kripi _(actors: Sharadvat, Janapadi (apsara), Kripa, Kripi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/kripa.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi Parva ~129-130)
- **Birth of Krishna Dvaipayana Vyasa** — Vyasa is born on a river island to sage Parashara and Satyavati, dark-complexioned and regarded as a partial amsha incarnation of Vishnu _(actors: Vyasa, Parashara, Satyavati)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/03-dvapara-yuga.md) — (Mahabharata Adi Parva; Vishnu Purana 3.3-3.4)
- **Birth of Kuru to Saṃvaraṇa and Tapatī** — Kuru is born to the Pauravā king Saṃvaraṇa and Tapatī, daughter of Sūrya, grafting a solar strand onto the Lunar line _(actors: Kuru, Saṃvaraṇa, Tapatī, Sūrya/Vivasvān)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kuru-the-eponym.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva (Caitraratha-parvan ~167–173); Viṣṇu Purāṇa IV.19)
- **Birth of Pradyumna as Kāmadeva reborn** — Rukmiṇī bears Kṛṣṇa's eldest son at Dwārakā, declared to be the god of love Kāma re-embodied after Śiva burnt him _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Rukmiṇī, Pradyumna (Kāmadeva))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/pradyumna.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.55; Harivaṃśa; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5)
- **Birth of Shishupala with three eyes and four arms** — Shishupala, son of Damaghosha and Shrutashrava, is born deformed with an extra eye and two extra arms, his abandonment forbidden by an aerial voice _(actors: Shishupala, Shrutashrava, Damaghosha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shishupala.md) — (Mahabharata Sabha-parva 40)
- **Birth of Vikarṇa among the hundred Kauravas** — Vikarṇa, third son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra and Gāndhārī, ripens from the māṃsapeśī flesh-ball split by Vyāsa, born an aṃśa of the Paulastya rākṣasas _(actors: Vikarṇa, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Gāndhārī, Vyāsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/vikarna.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva ~107 (Sambhava); Aṃśāvataraṇa ~1.61)
- **Birth of Vyasa** — Krishna Dvaipayana Vyasa is born on a Yamuna island to Parashara and Satyavati, a partial amsha of Vishnu _(actors: Vyasa, Parashara, Satyavati)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/03-dvapara-yuga.md) — (Mahabharata Adi Parva; Vishnu Purana 3.3-3.4)
- **Birth of Yuyutsu from the vaishya serving-woman** — Yuyutsu is born to Dhritarashtra by a vaishya serving-woman (Sughada/Sauvali) during Gandhari's failed two-year pregnancy, set apart from the hundred Kauravas by birth _(actors: Dhritarashtra, Sughada/Sauvali (vaishya maid), Gandhari)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/yuyutsu.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi Parva (Sambhava-parva ~115))
- **Boon of Durvasa to Kunti** — The sage Durvasa, pleased by Kunti's year of service, grants her a mantra to summon any deity and beget a child sharing his nature _(actors: Durvasa, Kunti)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/karna.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva ~305; Adi Parva 1.104)
- **Boyhood friendship with Droṇa and the half-kingdom vow** — Young Drupada, schooled beside Droṇa at Bharadvāja's āśrama, vows to share half his future kingdom with him _(actors: Drupada, Droṇa, Bharadvāja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/drupada.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva ~129)
- **Bride-price marriage of Mādrī to Pāṇḍu** — Bhīṣma secures the Madra princess Mādrī as Pāṇḍu's second queen by paying the customary bride-price (śulka) to her people _(actors: Mādrī, Bhīṣma, Pāṇḍu, Śalya, the Madra people)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/madri.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Building of Indraprastha** — Granted the wild tract Khāṇḍavaprastha, the Pāṇḍavas transform it into the splendid capital Indraprastha, its assembly-hall built by the asura architect Maya _(actors: Yudhiṣṭhira, Pāṇḍavas, Maya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/yudhishthira.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi/Sabhā Parva)
- **Composition of the Bhagavata Purana** — Narada finds Vyasa unsatisfied after dividing the Veda and composing the Mahabharata and prescribes singing Hari's lila, leading Vyasa to compose the Bhagavata Purana _(actors: Vyasa, Narada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavata-tales-catalog.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 1.4-6)
- **Curse of Mahābhiṣa and Gaṅgā** — King Mahābhiṣa and the goddess Gaṅgā exchange desire in Brahmā's court and are cursed to mortal birth, fixing Mahābhiṣa's rebirth as Śāntanu _(actors: Mahābhiṣa (future Śāntanu), Gaṅgā, Brahmā)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/shantanu.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva (Saṃbhava-parvan ~96))
- **Death of Shalya** — On Day 18 Shalya rampages as fourth commander until Yudhishthira, fated to kill him, hurls a mantra-consecrated dart that pierces his chest, ending the Madra resistance _(actors: Shalya, Yudhishthira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/09-shalya-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Shalya Parva I (Shalya-vadha))
- **Departure for the Kamyaka forest and the Akshaya-patra** — The exiles leave Hastinapura for the Kamyaka forest, and Surya grants Yudhishthira the inexhaustible vessel that yields unlimited food daily _(actors: the Pandavas, Draupadi, Surya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/03-vana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva 1-10)
- **Descent of portions (amshavatarana)** — Devas and asuras incarnate on earth to relieve its burden, with key heroes declared portions of gods _(actors: devas, asuras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/01-adi-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Adi Parva 59-64)
- **Devāpi's birth as eldest son of Pratīpa** — Devāpi is born firstborn of King Pratīpa of the Kuru line, ahead of brothers Bāhlīka and Śāntanu, making him rightful heir to Hastināpura _(actors: Devāpi, Pratīpa, Bāhlīka, Śāntanu)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/devapi.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva; Viṣṇu Purāṇa IV.20; Bhāgavata Purāṇa IX.22)
- **Dhṛtarāṣṭra's sleeplessness and summons of Vidura** — Tormented by foreboding of war over the Pāṇḍavas' kingdom, the blind king cannot sleep and summons Vidura, framing the Prajāgara discourse _(actors: Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Vidura)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vidura-niti.md) — (Mahābhārata Udyoga Parva 33)
- **Droṇa rejects Ekalavya** — The Niṣāda prince Ekalavya approaches Droṇa for archery training and is refused on account of his low birth and Droṇa's vow that none would surpass Arjuna _(actors: Ekalavya, Droṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ekalavya.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva)
- **Drona consecrated as Kaurava commander** — On Bhishma's counsel Duryodhana installs Drona as commander-in-chief; Drona promises to capture Yudhishthira alive if Arjuna can be drawn away _(actors: Drona, Duryodhana, Bhishma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/07-drona-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Drona Parva 1-16 (Dronabhisheka))
- **Duryodhana consecrates Ashvatthaman as final commander** — The three surviving Kaurava warriors reach the dying Duryodhana, who with his last strength consecrates Ashvatthaman as the final commander-in-chief _(actors: Duryodhana, Ashvatthaman, Kripa, Kritavarman)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/10-sauptika-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Sauptika Parva 1)
- **Earth appeals to the gods and Viṣṇu plucks two hairs** — Oppressed by demonic kings, Earth appeals to the gods; Viṣṇu agrees to descend, and the plucking of a dark and a light hair becomes Krishna and Balarāma _(actors: Bhūmi/Pṛthivī, Viṣṇu, Krishna, Balarāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/harivamsha.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva)
- **Ekalavya Surrenders His Thumb** — The Nishada prince Ekalavya learns archery before Drona's clay image and surrenders his right thumb as guru-dakshina _(actors: Ekalavya, Drona)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/README.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi Parva)
- **Entry into Kāmyaka-vana** — The Pāṇḍavas leave Hāstinapura after the dice-game and settle first in Kāmyaka on the Sarasvatī's desert-edge, attended by Dhaumya and brahmins _(actors: Yudhiṣṭhira, Bhīma, Arjuna, Nakula, Sahadeva, Draupadī, Dhaumya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kamyaka-dvaita-pandava-exile-forests.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva 5, 36)
- **Eternal Vrindavana rasa-lila (scriptural backdrop)** — Krishna and the gopis dance the nightly rasa-lila in Vrindavana, held in tradition to continue eternally in the Nidhivana groves _(actors: Krishna, the gopis, Radha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/banke-bihari-vrindavan-nidhivan.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 10.29-33; Braj sthala tradition)
- **Fire-birth of Dhṛṣṭadyumna** — Born full-grown and armed from Drupada's putrakāmeṣṭi revenge-sacrifice, named by a sky-voice as the fated slayer of Droṇa _(actors: Dhṛṣṭadyumna, Drupada, Yāja, Upayāja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/dhrishtadyumna.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva ~155-169)
- **Gāndhārī's curse on Krishna** — The bereaved Kuru queen Gāndhārī, having lost all hundred sons, curses Krishna that his Vrishni-Andhaka kinsmen will perish in fratricidal strife and he himself will die ignobly alone in the wilderness 36 years hence _(actors: Gāndhārī, Krishna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/16-mausala-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 16.1 (Mausala); Stri Parva 11)
- **Govardhana-dhāraṇa līlā** — Boy Kṛṣṇa lifts Govardhana hill on his little finger for seven days to shelter Braj from Indra's deluge — the act the Nathdwārā icon eternally depicts _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Indra, Nanda and the cowherds, cows of Braj)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/nathdwara-shrinathji.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.24–27)
- **Indra and Agni test King Shibi (dove and hawk)** — Indra as a hawk and Agni as a dove fall upon King Shibi, testing whether he will protect a refugee at any cost _(actors: Indra, Agni, Shibi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shibi.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva)
- **Jarasandha's seventeen sieges of Mathura** — Jarasandha of Magadha, Kamsa's father-in-law, besieges Mathura seventeen times to avenge Kamsa and is repelled each time, Krishna deliberately sparing him _(actors: Jarasandha, Krishna, Balarama, Yadavas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/muchukunda-and-kalayavana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana X.50; Vishnu Purana V.22; Harivamsha (Vishnu Parva))
- **Kṛṣṇa commissions Uddhava as envoy to Vraja** — From Mathurā, Kṛṣṇa sends his cousin and minister Uddhava to console the grieving cowherds and gopīs of Vṛndāvana with a consoling message _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Uddhava)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhramara-gita-uddhava-sandesha.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.46.1–11)
- **Karna consecrated as third Kaurava commander** — With Bhishma and Drona fallen, Duryodhana installs Karna as the third commander-in-chief; Karna vows to slay Arjuna or die and demands Shalya as a charioteer equal to Krishna _(actors: Duryodhana, Karna, Shalya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/08-karna-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Karna Parva 6-32)
- **Kaṃsa dispatches the last wave of demons to Vraja** — Forewarned the Vraja boy is his foretold killer, Kaṃsa sends his final relay of asuras — the bull Ariṣṭa and the horse Keśin — to Vṛndāvana _(actors: Kaṃsa, Ariṣṭāsura, Keśin)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/keshi-arishta-keshava.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa X.36–37; Harivaṃśa (Viṣṇu Parva); Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5)
- **Kṛṣṇa slays Mura at the Prāgjyotiṣa moat-fortress** — Kṛṣṇa, attacking Narakāsura's capital Prāgjyotiṣa/Kāmarūpa, first confronts and slays the thousand-armed (five/seven-headed) Dānava general Mura, who guards the moat-fortress with his many sons; Kṛṣṇa decapitates/destroys him with the Sudarśana chakra immediately before reaching Narakāsura. This slaying yields Viṣṇu/Kṛṣṇa's devotional epithets Murāri, Murahara, and Murabhid _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Mura (Murāsura), Mura's sons, Narakāsura)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/asura-wars-and-roster.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.59; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.29)
- **King Vareṇya's question to Gajānana** — Troubled by worldly sorrow and the problem of right action, King Vareṇya asks the Gajānana incarnation of Gaṇeśa for the highest knowledge, mirroring Arjuna's despondency _(actors: Gajānana (Gaṇeśa), King Vareṇya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ganesha-gita.md) — (Gaṇeśa Purāṇa, Krīḍā-khaṇḍa ch. 138)
- **King Śveta's self-directed giving** — A righteous, generous king performs every rite and gives lavishly but heaps all his gifts and feasts upon himself, never feeding guests or the needy _(actors: King Śveta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dana-upakhyanas-shveta-and-the-mongoose.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva (Tīrtha-yātrā / Mārkaṇḍeya block))
- **Kāliya's flight to the Yamunā** — The nāga Kāliya, fleeing Garuḍa from Ramaṇaka Dvīpa, takes refuge in a Yamunā pool barred to Garuḍa by Saubhari's curse _(actors: Kāliya, Garuḍa, sage Saubhari)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kaliya-mardana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.16; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5; Harivaṃśa)
- **Krishna curses Ashvatthama to deathless wandering** — After the night-massacre and the Brahmastra aimed at Uttara's womb, Krishna revives Parikshit and curses Ashvatthama to wander 3,000 years suffering until Kali's end _(actors: Krishna, Ashvatthama, Parikshit)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/the-chiranjivis.md) — (Mahabharata Sauptika Parva)
- **Krishna Narrates the Vrikasura Tale** — Krishna tells Yudhishthira the cautionary tale of Vrikasura after the Rajasuya/Ashvamedha sacrifices, answering why Shiva-worshippers gain wealth _(actors: Krishna, Yudhishthira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhasmasura-and-vrikasura.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 10.88.6-7)
- **Krishna's Flight West and Founding of Dvaraka** — Harried repeatedly by Jarasandha and threatened by Kalayavana, Krishna abandons Mathura and leads the Yadava clans west to the sea-girt safety of Saurashtra, earning the epithet Ranchhod _(actors: Krishna, Jarasandha, Kalayavana, Yadavas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dwarka-bet-dwarka-saurashtra.md) — (Bhagavata 10.50-52; Vishnu Purana 5.23; Harivamsha)
- **Kṛtavarma's lineage as a Bhoja-Vṛṣṇi chief** — Kṛtavarma, son of Hṛdika (hence Hārdikya), is a Bhoja-branch chief of the Vṛṣṇi/Yādava confederacy of Dvārakā and kinsman of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma _(actors: Kṛtavarma, Hṛdika, Kṛṣṇa, Balarāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/kritavarma.md) — (Mahābhārata; Bhāgavata Purāṇa IX.24)
- **Kuntī adopted by Kuntibhoja** — Śūrasena gives his firstborn daughter Pṛthā in adoption to his childless cousin Kuntibhoja, who renames her Kuntī and rears her _(actors: Śūrasena, Pṛthā/Kuntī, Kuntibhoja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/kunti.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Maṇḍavya's impalement and his curse on Yama** — Falsely accused, the silent ascetic Maṇḍavya is impaled but survives; learning his torment stemmed from a boyhood insect-piercing, he reforms cosmic law on the age of innocence and curses Yama to be born among men as a Śūdra _(actors: Aṇī-Maṇḍavya, Yama/Dharma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/vidura.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva CVIII)
- **Markandeya tells the Ramopakhyana to console Yudhishthira** — After Jayadratha's attempt to abduct Draupadi, the deathless sage Markandeya narrates the whole Rama story to console the humiliated Yudhishthira that even greater men suffered and recovered _(actors: Markandeya, Yudhishthira, Jayadratha, Draupadi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramopakhyana.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva 257)
- **Marriage and self-blindfolding** — Gāndhārī of Gandhāra weds the blind Dhṛtarāṣṭra and binds her own eyes for life to share his darkness _(actors: Gāndhārī, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Bhīṣma, Śakuni)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/gandhari.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva)
- **Maya builds the illusory assembly hall** — In gratitude, Maya raises a peerless hall of optical illusion at Indraprastha with materials from Lake Bindusaras _(actors: Maya Danava, Yudhishthira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/02-sabha-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Sabha Parva 1-3)
- **Mārkaṇḍeya narrates the Dharma-vyādha tale** — During the forest exile Mārkaṇḍeya answers Yudhiṣṭhira on whether women and the low-born can be wise by telling the story of Kauśika and the butcher _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyadha-gita.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva, Mārkaṇḍeya-samasyā)
- **Mudgala lives by uñchavṛtti at Kurukṣetra** — The brāhmaṇa-sage Mudgala subsists only on gleaned fallen grain, owning nothing in reserve, sustaining a fortnightly iṣṭi sacrifice and his family _(actors: Mudgala, his wife, his son)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mudgala.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva 246)
- **Mudgala's uñchavṛtti livelihood** — The brāhmaṇa Mudgala of Kurukṣetra subsists solely by gleaning fallen grain, yet performs the new/full-moon Iṣṭi and feeds gods, ancestors and guests before himself _(actors: Mudgala, his wife and son)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mudgala.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva, Mārkaṇḍeya-samāsya (Mudgala-Upākhyāna), ~258-260 / CE ~246-247)
- **Naraka's tyranny and theft from heaven** — Bhaumāsura plunders the worlds, stealing Aditi's earrings, Varuṇa's parasol and the Maṇi-parvata jewel-seat and imprisoning 16,100 royal women _(actors: Narakāsura (Bhauma), Aditi, Varuṇa, the captive women)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narakasura-vadha.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa X.59; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5)
- **Onset of autumn (śarad ṛtu-varṇana)** — The rains cease and autumn clears the skies, waters and groves of Vṛndāvana, setting the stage for the flute-song _(actors: Vṛndāvana / nature)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/venu-gita.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.20)
- **Pandavas choose Matsya for ajnata-vasa** — As the 12 forest years close, the Pandavas decide to spend their 13th incognito year at King Virata's prosperous kingdom of Matsya _(actors: Yudhishthira, Pandava brothers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/04-virata-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Virata Parva 1)
- **Pandavas take service at Virata's Matsya court** — The five Pandavas and Draupadi pass their thirteenth incognito year disguised as servants in King Virata's prosperous, dharmic kingdom of Matsya _(actors: Virata, Yudhishthira, Bhima, Arjuna, Nakula, Sahadeva, Draupadi, Sudeshna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/virata-and-his-children.md) — (Mahabharata, Virata Parva 1-5)
- **Piṅgalā's night of futile waiting** — Piṅgalā, a courtesan of Mithilā, waits adorned at her door all night for a wealthy patron who never comes _(actors: Piṅgalā (courtesan of Mithilā); the awaited wealthy lover)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pingala-gita.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva, Mokṣa-dharma ~XII.168)
- **Pārijāta-haraṇa (Kṛṣṇa carries off the Pārijāta tree from Nandana)** — To please his queen Satyabhāmā, Kṛṣṇa uproots the Pārijāta (coral/night-jasmine) wish-tree from Indra's celestial pleasure-grove Nandana in Amarāvatī and carries it to Dvārakā, defeating Indra who pursues to recover it _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Satyabhāmā, Indra)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/meru-kailash-and-cosmic-mountains.md) — (Harivaṃśa; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.30; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.59)
- **Pūtanā and the poison-breast (and her mokṣa)** — The child-killing demoness sent by Kaṃsa suckles the infant Krishna with a poisoned breast and is drained of life, yet is granted liberation for coming in the form of a mother _(actors: Krishna (infant), Pūtanā, Kaṃsa, Yaśodā, gopīs)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/krishna-balya-lila.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa X.6; Harivaṃśa; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5)
- **Pūtanā–Baka–Agha sibling triad** — The Bhāgavata binds the demoness Pūtanā, the crane Bakāsura, and the python Aghāsura into one demon-family, motivating Aghāsura's revenge _(actors: Pūtanā, Bakāsura, Aghāsura, Kaṃsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vraja-boyhood-demons.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa X.6, X.11–12; Harivaṃśa; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5)
- **Raivataka festival & the abduction of Subhadrā** — At the Vṛṣṇi-Andhaka spring festival on the Raivataka slopes, Arjuna abducts Subhadrā on Kṛṣṇa's counsel _(actors: Arjuna, Kṛṣṇa, Subhadrā, Vṛṣṇi-Andhaka Yādavas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva ~218-223; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10)
- **Rukmī blocks the match and pledges Rukmiṇī to Śiśupāla** — Rukmiṇī's arrogant brother Rukmī, hating Kṛṣṇa, vetoes the desired marriage and betroths her to Śiśupāla, ally of Jarāsandha _(actors: Rukmī, Bhīṣmaka, Rukmiṇī, Śiśupāla, Jarāsandha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rukmini-harana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.52; Harivaṃśa Viṣṇu-parva; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.26)
- **Sāmba is born of the Śaiva boon** — Jāmbavatī bears Sāmba, the son "won from Śiva" through Upamanyu's initiation and Kṛṣṇa's tapas _(actors: Jāmbavatī, Sāmba, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/upamanyu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva)
- **Sāmba's capture and the dragging of Hastināpura** — Sāmba abducts Duryodhana's daughter Lakṣmaṇā and is captured; Balarāma hooks his plough into Hastināpura's ramparts and drags the city toward the Gaṅgā until the Kurus release the couple _(actors: Sāmba, Lakṣmaṇā, Balarāma, Duryodhana, the Kuru champions)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/krishna-lila.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.68)
- **Sañjaya as Dhṛtarāṣṭra's court sūta and truth-teller** — The sūta Sañjaya Gāvalgaṇi serves as the blind king's charioteer-bard, minister, and confidant, openly blaming Dhṛtarāṣṭra's partiality and Duryodhana's greed for the coming war _(actors: Sañjaya, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Duryodhana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/sanjaya.md) — (Mahābhārata, Sabhā / Udyoga Parvas)
- **Sanjaya granted divine sight** — Vyasa grants the charioteer-bard Sanjaya the eye of the gods (divya-drishti) so he can narrate the entire war to the blind king Dhritarashtra _(actors: Vyasa, Sanjaya, Dhritarashtra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/06-bhishma-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Bhishma Parva 2)
- **Sāmba's curse and leprosy** — Sāmba, Kṛṣṇa's son by Jāmbavatī, is cursed (via Nārada's contrivance) and afflicted with leprosy _(actors: Sāmba, Kṛṣṇa, Jāmbavatī, Nārada)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/konark-sun-temple-arka-kshetra.md) — (Sāmba Purāṇa; Bhaviṣya Purāṇa)
- **Sāmba's leprosy and cure by Sūrya** — Sāmba, son of Kṛṣṇa, is cursed with leprosy (by Durvāsā or Nārada), performs a twelve-year penance to Sūrya, recovers a Sun-image from the Candrabhāgā river, installs it, and is healed _(actors: Sāmba, Kṛṣṇa, Sūrya, Durvāsā, Nārada)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/bhavishya-purana.md) — (Bhaviṣya Purāṇa (Brahma Parva); Sāmba Purāṇa; Varāha Purāṇa)
- **Śaṅkhacūḍa the guhyaka scatters the gopīs** — a wealthy Yakṣa follower of Kubera, drunk on pride, drives the women of Vraja away northward before the eyes of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma _(actors: Śaṅkhacūḍa the Yakṣa, the gopīs, Kṛṣṇa, Balarāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sudarshana-shankhachuda-vyoma.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.34.20–24)
- **Satyabhāmā's former birth as Guṇavatī** — at Dvārakā Kṛṣṇa reveals that his queen won him as husband by the merit of a flawless Kārtika-vrata kept in an earlier life as the poor brāhmaṇa girl Guṇavatī _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Satyabhāmā, Guṇavatī, Satrājit)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kartika-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma P., Uttara-khaṇḍa; Skanda P., Kārttikamāsa-māhātmya)
- **Satyabhāmā's Puṇyaka-vrata** — to keep Kṛṣṇa across lifetimes Satyabhāmā performs the Pārijāta-requiring vow, formally gifting Kṛṣṇa away to Nārada and then redeeming him by his weight in gold and gems _(actors: Satyabhāmā, Nārada, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/parijata-harana.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva 78 ff.)
- **Sātyaki beheads the fasting Bhūriśravas** — After Arjuna's arrow severs Bhūriśravas's sword-arm mid-duel, Sātyaki beheads the disarmed warrior seated in prāya, a breach of the war-code both armies condemn _(actors: Sātyaki, Bhūriśravas, Arjuna, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/satyaki.md) — (Mahābhārata 7.142–143, Droṇa Parva)
- **Sātyaki dies in the Mausala fratricide at Prabhāsa** — Taunting Kṛtavarma over the Sauptika massacre, Sātyaki beheads him and is then killed by the Bhoja-Andhaka mob with Pradyumna at his side, igniting the Yādava self-slaughter _(actors: Sātyaki, Kṛtavarma, Pradyumna, the Bhojas and Andhakas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/satyaki.md) — (Mahābhārata, Mausala Parva 3; Bhāgavata Purāṇa XI.30)
- **Sātyaki kills Śalva and spares Sañjaya** — In the final rout Sātyaki slays the mleccha king Śalva and captures Sañjaya, releasing him at Vyāsa's intervention so the epic's eyewitness survives _(actors: Sātyaki, Śalva, Sañjaya, Vyāsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/satyaki.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śalya Parva)
- **Sātyaki named among the seven Pāṇḍava marshals** — Yudhiṣṭhira appoints seven leaders over the seven Pāṇḍava akṣauhiṇīs, Sātyaki among them, while his kinsman Kṛtavarma leads Yādavas for the Kauravas _(actors: Sātyaki, Yudhiṣṭhira, Dhṛṣṭadyumna, Kṛtavarma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/satyaki.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva)
- **Sātyaki rebuts Balarāma's peace counsel** — At the Upaplavya war-council Sātyaki angrily answers Balarāma's conciliatory speech, declaring war the only reply to the dice-hall outrage _(actors: Sātyaki, Balarāma, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/satyaki.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva)
