- **Yudhishthira performs the Ashvamedha horse-sacrifice** — To expiate the slaughter of the war, Yudhishthira performs the ashvamedha (horse-sacrifice) that gives Book 14 of the Mahabharata its name _(actors: Yudhishthira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/anugita.md) — (Mahabharata, Ashvamedhika Parva)
- **Nalakūbara and Maṇigrīva cursed to become trees** — Kubera's sons Nalakūbara and Maṇigrīva are cursed by Nārada to become trees, later liberated by the infant Kṛṣṇa in the yamalārjuna episode _(actors: Nalakūbara, Maṇigrīva, Nārada, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/yakshas-and-guhyakas.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.10)
- **The Yakṣa Praśna and revival of the Pāṇḍavas** — A yakṣa guarding a lake kills the four younger Pāṇḍavas who drink without answering him; Yudhiṣṭhira alone answers the dharma-catechism, and the yakṣa — revealed as Yama-Dharma — restores his brothers to life _(actors: Yudhiṣṭhira, Yama-Dharma, Pāṇḍavas)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/yakshas-and-guhyakas.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva)
- **Sthūṇa exchanges sex with Śikhaṇḍin** — The yakṣa Sthūṇa compassionately exchanges his sex with Śikhaṇḍinī so she can become male; Kubera curses the exchange permanent, enabling the fall of Bhīṣma _(actors: Sthūṇa, Śikhaṇḍin, Kubera, Bhīṣma)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/yakshas-and-guhyakas.md) — (Mahābhārata Udyoga Parva)
- **Astika halts Janamejaya's snake-sacrifice (Nag Panchami)** — The sage Astika stops King Janamejaya's sarpa-satra and saves the serpent race, the Adi Parva frame-story commemorated at Nag Panchami _(actors: Astika, Janamejaya)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi Parva)
- **Krishna subdues the serpent Kaliya** — Krishna subdues the serpent Kaliya in the Yamuna, recalled in western Nag Panchami strands _(actors: Krishna, Kaliya)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Krishna Lila)
- **Birth of Krishna (Janmashtami)** — Krishna, the 8th avatar, is born at midnight in Mathura's prison to Devaki and Vasudeva, commemorated by Krishna Janmashtami _(actors: Krishna, Devaki, Vasudeva)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Krishna Lila; Bhagavata Purana)
- **Krishna lifts Govardhan Hill (Govardhan Puja)** — Krishna lifts Govardhan Hill on one finger to shelter Vrindavan from Indra's rains, teaching worship of nature over Indra; commemorated as Annakut _(actors: Krishna, Indra)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Bhagavata Purana)
- **Vyasa dictates the Mahabharata to Ganesha** — Ganesha serves as scribe to whom Vyasa dictates the Mahabharata, invoked at Ganesh Chaturthi as lord of letters and beginnings _(actors: Vyasa, Ganesha)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Mahabharata tradition)
- **Bhishma chooses death at Uttarayana (Makar Sankranti)** — Bhishma, lying on his bed of arrows with the boon of icchamrityu, waits for Uttarayana before releasing his life, commemorated at Makar Sankranti _(actors: Bhishma)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Mahabharata, Anushasana Parva)
- **Draupadi binds her sari on Krishna's finger** — Draupadi binds her torn sari on Krishna's bleeding finger and he vows lifelong protection, repaid at the vastra-haran; a Raksha Bandhan charter _(actors: Draupadi, Krishna)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Mahabharata, Sabha Parva)
- **Krishna speaks the Gita on Mokshada Ekadashi** — Vaikuntha/Mokshada Ekadashi marks the day the Bhagavad Gita was spoken by Krishna to Arjuna _(actors: Krishna, Arjuna)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Bhagavad Gita tradition)
- **Birth of Vyasa / division of the Veda (Guru Purnima)** — Guru Purnima honors the birth and Veda-division day of Veda Vyasa, the Adi Guru who split the one Veda into four and composed the Mahabharata and Puranas _(actors: Veda Vyasa)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Vyasa tradition)
- **The Avantī Brāhmaṇa's Song of Forbearance (Bhikṣu-gītā)** — Recited by Kṛṣṇa to Uddhava in his farewell teaching: a rich but miserly brāhmaṇa of Avantī (Ujjain), who hoarded wealth and neglected gods, guests, and ancestors, is stripped of everything by fate (relatives, thieves, time, fire, accident); reduced to begging he is mocked, beaten, and reviled by the townsfolk, yet bears it without anger. Reflecting, he realizes that neither other people, the body, the planets (graha), time (kāla), nor fate/karma cause his suffering — the mind alone (manas) is the cause of bondage and liberation; he vows forbearance (titikṣā), crosses the ocean of material existence, and attains peace _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Uddhava, the unnamed miserly brāhmaṇa of Avantī (Ujjain), the townspeople of Avantī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/uddhava-gita.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 11.23 (Bhikṣu-gītā / Avantī-brāhmaṇa-gītā), within the Uddhava-gītā (11.6.40–11.29))
